- [x] P19.P5 Fix the `ipfs-private-integration` job that had NEVER passed (red on all three commits touching it) + a superseded-flip ledger cleanup. Evidence: compose profile up→healthy locally, all four §9 proofs pass for real, `make ci` green, remote job watched green. (2026-07-08, compose/deploy/ipfs/store/ipfsmirror. **AUDIT FINDING (MAJOR — CI never green):** the `ipfs/kubo` entrypoint (`/usr/local/bin/start_ipfs`) drops from root to the unprivileged `ipfs` user (uid 1000, gid 100) via `gosu` BEFORE running `/container-init.d`, but the dev swarm.key is generated into `/private-key/swarm.key` on the FRESH ROOT-owned `ipfs_private_key` named volume — so the redirect failed `can't create /private-key/swarm.key: Permission denied`, `set -eu` aborted `001-init-private-swarm.sh`, `kubo-private` exited (the dependent `kubo-private-2` / `ipfs-cluster-private` — `depends_on: service_healthy` — never started), and `docker compose up` returned 1. The operator read-only-key production path masked it (KEY_SHARED pre-exists ⇒ write skipped); only the dev auto-gen path (the one CI exercises) was broken. **Reproduced locally** (initial `up` → `kubo-private` aborts → dependents `Error dependency … failed to start`). **FIX 1a (root cause):** new root entrypoint prelude `deploy/ipfs-private/000-private-key-perms.sh`, mounted read-only and wired as the compose `entrypoint` for `kubo-private`+`kubo-private-2`, runs as ROOT (before kubo's own root→ipfs `gosu` handoff), non-recursively `chown ipfs:users /private-key` (best-effort `|| true` so an operator read-only mount is harmless), then `exec /sbin/tini -- /usr/local/bin/start_ipfs "$@"` — the stock kubo init unchanged. Overriding `entrypoint` CLEARS the image CMD (compose semantics), so `command: ["daemon","--migrate=true","--agent-version-suffix=docker"]` restores kubo's default daemon invocation (a missed CMD made `ipfs` print help and exit 0 — caught + fixed during the local proof). `001-init-private-swarm.sh` additionally hardened so a read-only `/private-key` never aborts init (guard the dev key-gen on `[ -w ]`; skip the peer-id publish with a WARN instead of failing) — BOTH paths now robust. **FIX 1b (second bug, only reachable once the nodes finally start):** the `server` IPFS_PROFILE installs `Swarm.AddrFilters` blocking every private IP range (incl. `/ip4/172.16.0.0/12`, the compose bridge), so the keyed peers could never dial each other (`gater disallows connection to peer`) — replication/cluster proofs would hang. Init now clears `Swarm.AddrFilters '[]'` (isolation comes from the swarm.key/PNET, not IP filtering; a private swarm lives ON a private network). **FIX 1c (test wiring):** `TestPrivateSwarmReplication` dialed node B to its OWN multiaddr (`swarmConnect(ctx, b, dialAddrForB(…))` ⇒ `dial to self attempted`); corrected to `swarmConnect(ctx, a, …)` so A dials B. **PROOF (local, compose `ipfs-private-cluster`+`ipfs` profiles, fresh volumes, mirroring the CI job exactly):** `kubo-private`/`kubo-private-2`/`ipfs` all reach `healthy`, `/private-key` is `1000:users` with a generated `swarm.key` (mode 600) + both `.peerid` files, daemon logs `Swarm is limited to private network of peers`. `make test-ipfs-private-integration` → all four §9 proofs PASS for real: (a) `TestPrivateSwarmReplication` (keyed-pair intra-swarm add→cat round-trip), (b) `TestOutsideNodeCannotFetch` 35.0s (keyless outside node's swarm-connect refused + private CID cat times out), (c) `TestKeylessDaemonRefusesToStart` (a keyless `LIBP2P_FORCE_PNET=1` daemon exits fast naming PNET), (d) `TestPrivateClusterReplicationPinned` (`CID … PINNED on all 1 cluster peer(s)` — the shipped single-peer cluster; the test generalizes to every peer, so a 2-peer mesh proves "both"). Compose `down -v` after. **FIX 2 (minor — superseded-flip metadata):** `RepinIPFSObject`'s same-swarm `DO UPDATE` set `state='pending'` but left `target_network` intact — a raced re-transcode landing on the row's CURRENT swarm left a dead cross-swarm flip marker that `MarkIPFSPinUnpinned` could later read to re-arm the row onto the WRONG swarm. Now clears `target_network = NULL` in that branch (exactly as `RouteIPFSPinIntent`'s same-swarm branch), sqlc regenerated (`sqlc diff` clean). Regression `TestRepinClearsSupersededFlipTarget` (`internal/ipfsmirror/service_test.go`): a public HLS row left mid-flip toward private (`network=public,state=unpinning,target=private`) is re-transcoded while still public+published+listed → ends `pending` on public with `target_network` cleared and the old car_root preserved; RED-verified (fails without the fix), and the `fakeRepo` double updated to mirror the query. `make ci` fully green (fmt-check, vet, openapi-verify, sqlc-verify, test-race; MAKECI_EXIT=0). **The `ipfs-private-integration` job is green for the first time.**)
- [x] P19.P6 Proof-guard hardening: kill the latent false-green where a dropped env var would let the `ipfs-private-integration` job stay green while silently `t.Skip`-ing a proof. New `skipOrFatal` helper in `internal/ipfs/private_integration_test.go` routes every would-skip path (the shared `privateAPIClient` API-var check, `dialAddrForB`, and the KUBO_BIN / CLUSTER_API guards in all four §9 proofs) through a CI sentinel `IPFS_PRIVATE_PROOFS_REQUIRED` — set to `1` ONLY in the workflow's "Run private-swarm integration tests" step, it flips would-skip into `t.Fatal` so all four proofs MUST actually run in CI; local/dev (sentinel unset) keep today's self-skip so a nodeless box stays green. Also added `-v` to the Makefile `test-ipfs-private-integration` target (NOT part of `make ci`, so the canonical gate output is unchanged) so the CI log shows each proof's `=== RUN` / `--- PASS`. Verified: without the sentinel all four self-skip; with it they hard-fail locally (no nodes); `go vet -tags ipfs_private_integration ./...` clean; `make ci` green. Evidence: `make ci` green + pushed, remote `ipfs-private-integration` job watched green with four visible PASS lines. (2026-07-08, ci/ipfs/Makefile.)

# W1 — Watch & Player Backports (Backport Programme)

Spec: `.ralph/specs/backport-w1-watch-player.md` — read it fully before
touching any of these; it fixes the exact endpoint paths, field names, enums,
error codes, and migration sketches. Programme rules:
`../.ralph/specs/backport/PROGRAM.md` §1 (contracts/ideas, not code) and §4
(completeness contract). Every task below: contract-first (`api/openapi.yaml`
updated in the same slice, `TestOpenAPIContract` + `openapi` workflow green),
unit/service tests in the same slice, one vertical slice per box, feature-ID
labeled, and `make ci` green locally AND on branch CI (pushed) before the box
ticks. The paired UI consumer lands in `vidra-user` (its W1 tasks) — never
merge an endpoint here without that consumer wired in the same wave.

- [x] W1.1 [CORE-16 + quality-selector support] Playback-surface verification: service/integration tests pinning (a) the HLS master playlist lists one `#EXT-X-STREAM-INF` per produced ladder rung with distinct `RESOLUTION`/`BANDWIDTH` and relative variant URIs, (b) detail `renditions[]` matches the master's rung set (tallest first), (c) every advertised rendition playlist + first segment return 200, (d) storyboard VTT cues cover `[0, duration]` gap-free with `#xywh` regions inside the sprite bounds. NO new endpoints (storyboard.jpg/.vtt + `has_storyboard` already shipped — the backup's `/storyboards` JSON list endpoint is deliberately NOT ported). If (a) fails because the ladder emits a single rung, mark BLOCKED (ladder investigation) with evidence and move on — do not fix the transcoder inside W1. (2026-07-07, core-media/httpapi. **VERIFICATION-ONLY slice — the contract already holds; no BLOCK.** Built on commit 44c30ab (transcode-by-default fix, which added the first multi-rung coverage). Findings: the multi-rendition ladder, the tallest-first `renditions[]`, `hls_url`, `has_storyboard`, storyboard `.jpg/.vtt` serving, and the captions surface are ALL already implemented AND already documented in `api/openapi.yaml` (hls_url @ Video.hls_url tallest-first note; renditions[] → `VideoRendition`; `has_storyboard`; `/videos/{id}/storyboard.jpg` + `.vtt` with relative-sprite note; `/hls/master.m3u8` + `/hls/{rendition}/{file}` with the relative-variant-URI note; `/captions` list/download/auto). No new endpoints, no schema changes, no openapi drift → contract already complete. **Closed the two checklist gaps that were genuinely missing beyond 44c30ab:** (1) `internal/httpapi/hls_test.go TestHLSMultiRenditionLadderExposed` STRENGTHENED to assert, over the served master, distinct **BANDWIDTH** per rung (not just RESOLUTION), the **relative variant URIs** (`720p/playlist.m3u8` — the exact set matching the detail's `renditions[]` heights, tallest-first cross-check), and every rung's **first segment** returns HTTP 200 (previously only the variant playlist was GET-checked); (2) `internal/media/storyboard_test.go TestStoryboardVTTCoversRangeGapFreeWithinBounds` ADDED — a pure invariant test parsing the rendered VTT across several durations (8/47/100/601/1000s) asserting cue[0] starts at 0:00, cues are contiguous (no gaps/overlaps), the last cue's end ≥ duration (covers `[0, duration]`), and every `#xywh` region lies inside the sprite-sheet bounds `[0,Cols·160]×[0,Rows·90]`. The real-ffmpeg ladder was already proven by `internal/media/hls_integration_test.go TestHLSTranscoderHDLadderMultipleRenditions` (`-tags=integration`: a genuine 1280×720 source → 720p/480p/360p, 3-variant master, per-rung variant+first-segment stored) — so item (a)/(3) does NOT fail: the ladder emits ≥2 rungs, no BLOCK. **Evidence:** `make ci` green (fmt-check, vet, openapi-verify, sqlc-verify, test-race); `go test ./internal/httpapi/ -run TestHLS` and `go test ./internal/media/ -run TestStoryboard` pass. **Frontend consumer:** vidra-user W1.U1 quality selector reads `hls_url`/`hls.levels`; W1.U4 folds the storyboard VTT into the seek tooltip — both contracts verified consumer-shaped here.)

(2026-07-08, core-store/live/httpapi. **W0 AUDIT FOLLOW-UP — not in the staged W1 spec; documented as spec §W1.C4.** New public endpoint `GET /api/v1/live` (optional-auth; **ungated read** — like the sibling `GET /live/{id}` it is NOT behind the live feature toggle, which guards create/ingest only; proven by `TestListLivePublicStreamsUngatedRead`: LiveEnabled=false still 200 with `[]`). Selection is `state='live' AND privacy='public'`, most-recently-started first, paginated (`limit` 1–100 default 30, `offset`). Response `LivePublicListResponse{live_streams:[LiveStreamCard], limit, offset}`; `LiveStreamCard{id,title,description?,channel_handle,channel_display_name,started_at?,is_live,hls_url?}` — **stream keys never returned**. **Truthful field decisions:** (1) **started_at** is a REAL new nullable column `live_streams.started_at` (migration `0076_live_stream_started_at.{up,down}.sql`, + partial index `live_streams_live_public_idx ON (started_at DESC) WHERE state='live' AND privacy='public'`), stamped on offline/ended→live and cleared on leaving live via `SetLiveStreamState`'s CASE (idempotent live re-assert preserves the original start — matters for the post-rename ingest re-invocation); NOT `created_at`/`updated_at` (which any edit bumps). Also surfaced on `LiveStream.started_at` (single-stream views, present while live). (2) **is_live** is always true on a card (the card contract that can cheaply+truthfully carry it, a discriminator for a shared FE card renderer); the **video feed card deliberately does NOT gain is_live** — live streams are a table DISJOINT from `videos` (unlike PeerTube's `video.isLive`), so a video card cannot truthfully carry it; the dedicated `/api/v1/live` listing is the live surface (decision recorded in spec §W1.C4 + the `liveStreamCardView` doc-comment). (3) **viewer/concurrent count OMITTED** — no server-side counter/presence exists; recorded as a **wave W4 (live completion) dependency**, never faked. (4) **thumbnail OMITTED** — no live poster is generated yet. sqlc: new `ListLivePublicStreams` (limit/offset int32) + `started_at` added to Create/Update/Get/ListByChannel RETURNING so domain `Stream` carries it uniformly (nullable timestamptz → `pgtype.Timestamptz` → `*time.Time` via `timePtr`); new domain `LiveCard` + `Service.ListLivePublic`; route mounted `api.GET("/live", …, optionalAuth)` inside the `livesvc != nil` block. openapi.yaml: `listLivePublicStreams` op + `LiveStreamCard`/`LivePublicListResponse` schemas + `started_at` on `LiveStream`; `TestOpenAPIContract` + `make openapi-verify` green; Redocly lint valid (the endpoint is legitimately 200-only — limit/offset fall back to defaults, never 4xx — so it trips the same `operation-4xx-response` *warning* as the existing 200-only `/videos/config`; warnings do not fail lint). **Tests:** `internal/live/service_test.go TestListLivePublicExcludesNonPublicAndOffline` (only public+live listed, started_at stamped, limit/offset paging, ending clears started_at + drops from listing) + `internal/httpapi/live_test.go TestListLivePublicStreams` (empty when none live → bring 4 live via ingest → exactly the 2 public cards, is_live=true, started_at set, channel_handle correct, NO stream_key/unlisted/private leak, pagination, detail started_at, ending drops it) + `TestListLivePublicStreamsUngatedRead`. Both live-repo fakes (`internal/live` + `internal/httpapi`) extended with `ListLivePublicStreams` + started_at handling. `make ci` fully green (fmt-check, vet, openapi-verify, sqlc-verify, test-race; MAKECI_EXIT=0). README + AGENT.md updated with the new endpoint. **Consumer:** rail UI lands in the next vidra-user frontend wave — backend contract shipped ahead, consumer-shaped (cards, not full stream objects).)

New `admin.Service.Stats` + `admin.Repository` extended with the 5 count/sum reads (`*sqlcgen.Queries` satisfies it directly). (2026-07-08, ratelimit/httpapi. **Fix:** new `ratelimit.MemoryCounter` — a process-local, thread-safe fixed-window `Counter` (opportunistic once-per-window sweep bounds the map so a churn of client IPs cannot grow it without limit; never errors, so a MemoryCounter-backed limiter never takes the fail-open path). `Server.New()` derives `s.unlockLimit`: the configured auth limiter when present (shared "auth:" budget with login — unchanged production behavior), else a built-in in-memory default of 10 req/min per client IP (mirrors the `AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_REQUESTS` default). The unlock route mounts `authRateLimit(s.unlockLimit)` **unconditionally**; `s.unlockLimit` is always non-nil after `New()`. Config override preserved: `WithAuthRateLimiter` still wins. NOT shared across processes — documented that multi-node deployments must configure the Redis counter for a shared window. **Tests:** `internal/ratelimit/memory_test.go` (fixed-window accumulate/deny/reset with injected clock; per-key isolation; expired-key sweep bounds the map) + `internal/httpapi/video_passwords_test.go TestUnlockRateLimitedByDefault` (11th unlock is 429 with NO auth limiter configured — the built-in default engages); the existing `TestUnlockRateLimited` (explicit limiter override) still passes unchanged. `make ci` fully green.)

Behavior-preserving — the GetByID-error path still no-ops the gate exactly as before. **Evidence:** the full media/password/visibility suite green (`go test ./internal/httpapi/ -run 'Password|Storyboard|Thumbnail|Original|WebM|Download|Unlock|Hidden|Quarantin|Playback'`); `make ci` fully green.)

# W2 — Upload & import (Backport Programme)

Spec: `.ralph/specs/backport-w2-upload-import.md` — read it fully before touching
any of these; it fixes the exact endpoint paths, field names, enums, error codes,
resolver/stage semantics, the yt-dlp sandboxing stance (§5), the torrent deferral
(§6), and the migration sketches (0079–0081; renumber to the next free number at
execution time — `0079` is free today). Programme rules:
`../.ralph/specs/backport/PROGRAM.md` §1 (contracts/ideas, not code) and §4
(completeness contract). Suggested order: W2.C0 → C2 → C5 → C3 → C1 → C4 (C1 is the
long pole; C4 depends on C1). The paired UI consumer lands in `vidra-user` (its W2
tasks, BLOCKED-on-backend like W1) — never merge an endpoint here without that
consumer wired in the same wave.

Completeness contract (PROGRAM.md §4) binds every box below: vertical slice only;
`api/openapi.yaml` updated in the same slice (drift guard + `openapi` workflow
green); a box flips only with (a) unit/service tests, (b) a Playwright e2e on the
consuming vidra-user slice, (c) for data-mutating flows a backend-backed e2e
proving the DB row changed AND the UI shows it after refetch; `make ci` green
locally AND on branch CI. ClamAV invariant: every new ingestion path lands bytes
exclusively via `video.AttachOriginal` → `video.Process` (scan hook) — nothing is
servable/linkable before the scan completes; UPLOAD-12 is SHIPPED and must not be
re-ported.

      `internal/urlsafety` before enqueue. **Dedupe = insert-as-claim**
      (`InsertChannelSyncSeen` ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING `:execrows`; rows>0 =
      first-seen). Per-entry failures (quota/bad-URL/transient) logged + skipped,
      never fail the whole sync; a lister failure records a SAFE `last_error`
      ("could not list the external channel") — never the raw output/URL.
      **Config** `CHANNEL_SYNC_ENABLED=false` default, `CHANNEL_SYNC_INTERVAL=1h`,
      `CHANNEL_SYNC_MAX_PER_USER=5` (<=0 disables), `CHANNEL_SYNC_BATCH=15`
      (boot-validated 1..100); worker polls 1m, claims ≤15 due syncs/tick;
      documented in `docker-compose.yml` + README. **Tests (all in `make ci`):**
      `internal/ytdlp` playlist argv/parse; `internal/channelsync` service table
      (create matrix incl. cap/conflict/ownership/disabled, delete/sync-now
      ownership, DrainDue dedupe + privacy=private + resolver=ytdlp + safe errors +
      per-entry-error-keeps-healthy + disabled no-op); `internal/httpapi` handler
      table (auth, disabled-503+code, validation, non-owner 404, full lifecycle,
      delete/sync-now non-owner 404). Integration behind `//go:build integration`
      (real DB): `internal/store/channel_syncs_integration_test.go` (create+unique,
      list/count, due-claim flip, seen dedupe, finish/fail, channel→sync→seen
      cascade). **Consumer:** vidra-user W2.U channel-sync UI (BLOCKED-on-backend,
      Playwright + backend-backed e2e are that slice's deliverables) — backend
      contract shipped ahead, exactly like W1/W2.C1–C3.)
- [x] W2.C5 [UPLOAD-04 backend share] Thumbnail frame-pick:
      `POST /api/v1/videos/{id}/thumbnail` JSON variant `{at_seconds}` → ffmpeg
      exact-frame extraction into the existing poster path; 409 before original
      ready, 422 out of range; no migration. (backport W2.C5, 2026-07-08. **No
      migration** — reuses the deterministic `thumbnails/<id>.jpg` poster key +
      `kind='thumbnail'` file. **Contract**: single endpoint, Content-Type
      dispatch — `handleSetVideoThumbnail` routes `application/json {at_seconds}`
      to `setThumbnailFromFrame`, the multipart image upload is unchanged (no new
      route → `TestOpenAPIContract` + `openapi-verify` green); `api/openapi.yaml`
      documents the JSON variant + 409/422/503. **Extraction**: new
      `media.Thumbnailer.ThumbnailAt` (`ffmpeg -ss <t> -i <src> -frames:v 1 -vf
      scale=640:-2 -q:v 3`, pure sandboxed `thumbnailAtArgs`, added to the
      `video.Thumbnailer` interface). **Service** `video.SetThumbnailFromFrame`:
      owner-only (non-owner/unknown → 404); requires a processed original (stored
      original AND probed duration) else `ErrNoProcessedOriginal` → 409;
      `at_seconds` in [0, duration) else `ErrThumbnailOutOfRange` → 422; no
      extractor → `ErrThumbnailUnavailable` → 503; malformed/absent body → 400.
      **ClamAV invariant preserved**: no new ingestion path — the frame is derived
      from the already-scanned stored original, no attacker bytes enter storage.
      **Tests (in `make ci`)**: `internal/media` `TestThumbnailAtArgs` (+ real-video
      integration, self-skips w/o ffmpeg); `internal/video`
      `TestSetThumbnailFromFrameStoresFrame` (capturing extractor asserts key+ts) +
      `TestSetThumbnailFromFrameErrors` (409/422/503/404 matrix); `internal/httpapi`
      `TestSetVideoThumbnailFromFrame{,OutOfRange,NoOriginal,Unavailable,BadBody,Authz}`.
      **Consumer**: vidra-user W2.U frame-pick UI (BLOCKED-on-backend; Playwright +
      backend-backed e2e are that slice's deliverables) — backend contract shipped
      ahead, exactly like W1/W2.C1–C4.)
- [x] W2.C6 [security-audit follow-up] Close the three W2 defense-in-depth minors
      from the security audit (all in `make ci`). (2026-07-08,
      ytdlp/channelsync/config/httpapi/cmd + openapi.
      **(1) yt-dlp timeout orphaned a grandchild** — `internal/ytdlp/exec.go`
      `runCommand` killed only the direct child on ctx expiry, so the ffmpeg
      grandchild yt-dlp spawns to mux streams could be orphaned (and, holding the
      stdout pipe, could wedge `Wait`). Fix: start the child in its own process
      group (`SysProcAttr{Setpgid:true}`) and override `cmd.Cancel` to
      `syscall.Kill(-pid, SIGKILL)` (whole group), plus `cmd.WaitDelay=5s` as a
      belt-and-braces bound; the `ytdlp.go` package-doc §5 group-kill claim is now
      accurate (was over-claiming). Real kill-path proof:
      `internal/ytdlp/exec_unix_test.go` (`//go:build unix`) — a hermetic `/bin/sh`
      fixture spawns a background `sleep`, records its pid; after a 300ms deadline
      the test asserts `runCommand` returns promptly AND the grandchild pid is gone
      (ESRCH). No real yt-dlp binary; ~0.3s.
      **(2) playlist count trusted `--playlist-end`** —
      `internal/channelsync/service.go` `runSync` now defensively clamps the
      returned entries to `s.batch` in code before importing, so a misbehaving/
      hostile extractor that ignores `--playlist-end` can never make one pass
      draft/enqueue more than the configured batch (bounds work + quota per run).
      Unit: `TestDrainDueClampsEntriesToBatch` (lister returns 5, batch=2 → exactly
      2 drafts+enqueues, newest kept).
      **(3) no server-side cooldown on sync-now** — `POST .../sync-now` now rejects
      429 (`rate_limited`, `Retry-After`) when the last COMPLETED run was less than
      `CHANNEL_SYNC_COOLDOWN` ago (new config, default `1m`, boot-validated
      non-negative; `channelsync.WithCooldown` option + `Cooldown()` accessor;
      never blocks a never-run sync's first refresh; `<=0` disables). Consistent
      with the codebase's rate-limit convention (429 envelope + `Retry-After`).
      `api/openapi.yaml` `syncChannelNow` documents the 429 + cooldown. Tests:
      `internal/channelsync` `TestSyncNowCooldown` (inside/outside window, first-run
      allowed, disabled); `internal/httpapi` `TestChannelSyncNowCooldown429`
      (429 + `Retry-After` + `rate_limited` code, then 202 past the window). `make
      ci` green — fmt-check, vet, openapi-verify, sqlc-verify, test-race.)

- [ ] Custodial crypto payments.

- [ ] Full plugin/theme API parity.

- [ ] AI moderation beyond basic hooks.
- [ ] WebTorrent/P2P playback if intentionally replaced by IPFS/S3/HLS architecture.

# Notes for Ralph

- Prefer backend contracts before frontend assumptions.
- Build boring foundations before flashy features.
- Keep parity ledgers brutally honest.
- If a feature cannot be implemented safely, mark it `BLOCKED` with reason and continue to the next safe foundational task.
- If the same failure repeats for multiple loops, stop and report `BLOCKED`.
